========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, doctor. Thank you for seeing me. I, um... I hope I'm not wasting your time with this. It's a little embarrassing to talk about, to be honest. I mean, I've worked outdoors in agriculture for years, so I know I shouldn't be so hung up on this, but... well, I just can't seem to get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. 

It's the deep cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin. Whenever I have to go into town and talk to the banker or the county folks, I just feel like they're staring right at all of it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a mountain out of a molehill, that I just look like a normal man who's worked outside his whole life. And I want to believe her, I really do. But it just bothers me so much. Does that make sense, though? Coming in to see you for something like that? I hope it's okay that I'm bringing it up.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I mean, I still get all the daily chores done, I promise. I don't let it stop me from doing the actual physical work around the farm. 

But, um... I guess it has been getting in the way of the business side of things a bit. I feel a little foolish admitting this, to be honest, because I know I should just push through it. But there have been times recently where I've actually missed calls with our bankers, and I've put off some pretty important farm decisions. It's just... I'd spend so much time trying to get myself looking acceptable enough to sit down in a room with them, and when I couldn't, I'd just find an excuse to cancel. 

And we also have this little retreat on the property, and we do some craft sales... I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't stomach the idea of people looking at those pictures and seeing how awful my skin looks. 

I know it's not right to let it affect our livelihood like that, and I really am trying my best not to let it get out of hand. Is that... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm explaining things alright for you.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Well, I... I don't know, honestly. I mean, my wife tells me I'm crazy, that I just look like a guy who's been out in the sun his whole life. And I want to agree with her, I really do. I know I shouldn't care so much about it.

But there have been times recently where I've actually missed calls with our bankers, and I've put off some pretty important farm decisions. It's just... I'd spend so much time trying to get myself looking acceptable enough to sit down in a room with them, and when I couldn't, I'd just find an excuse to cancel. \n\nAnd we also have this little retreat on the property, and we do some craft sales... I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't stomach the idea of people looking at those pictures and seeing how awful my skin looks. \n\nI know it's not right to let it affect our livelihood like that, and I really am trying my best not to let it get out of hand. I hope I'm explaining things alright for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do, or is it more that you worry what others might think of them?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know, honestly. I mean, my wife tells me I'm crazy, that I just look like a guy who's been out in the sun his whole life. And I want to agree with her, I really do. I know I shouldn't care so much about it. \n\nBut when I look in the mirror, it's just... it looks so rough to me. The cracks are so deep. I guess I do worry a lot about what other people think, especially the folks in town who don't work with their hands like I do. I just feel like they must see it the same way I do, you know? Like I look worn out or... or unkempt. \n\nI don't mean to sound like I'm obsessed with what people think of me. I know I should have thicker skin than that, no pun intended. But it just feels so obvious to me. Does that... does that answer your question? And I want to believe her, I really do. I don't want to sit here and sound completely unreasonable to you. \n\nBut... well, I guess that's exactly what brings me in to see you. Even though I hear what she says, I just... I can't seem to get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. Well, um... I guess we're sort of circling around the same idea, aren't we? I... I don't want to waste your time repeating myself, I know you're very busy. 

But yes, I suppose... I suppose I do know, deep down, that my perception might be a little skewed. I mean, if I'm being perfectly honest with myself—and with you—I know that when I look in the mirror, I'm probably zooming in on every little flaw in a way that a stranger walking down the street just wouldn't do. 

It's just that the feeling is so strong, you know? Even when my brain tells me, "Hey, the banker isn't staring at your hands," my stomach is still tied in knots because it feels like he is. 

I'm trying to be better about recognizing that, though. I really am. I know it's not productive to get so worked up over it. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying to give you a straight answer, I just... I want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you have what you need.

Oh, um... that's a good question. I'm... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really kept a close eye on whether it changes from one day to the next.

Oh, well... that's a tough one. I mean, I certainly don't want to sit here and tell you that I'm completely shallow, you know? I mean, I've worked outdoors in agriculture for years, so I know I shouldn't be so hung up on this, but... well, I just can't seem to get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. \n\nIt's the deep cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin. Whenever I have to go into town and talk to the banker or the county folks, I just feel like they're staring right at all of it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a mountain out of a molehill, that I just look like a normal man who's worked outside his whole life. And I want to believe her, I really do. But it just bothers me so much. Does that make sense, though? Coming in to see you for something like that? I hope it's okay that I'm bringing it up.\n\nDoctor: Is it just your face and hands that you're concerned about, or are there other parts of your body that bother you as well?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's really just... just my face and my hands. The rest of me is, you know, fine. Just normal for a guy my age, I suppose. \n\nI'm not trying to complain about my whole body or anything, I promise. I know I'm 62, I'm not expecting to look perfect. I really hope I don't come across as too vain or... or self-obsessed by focusing on this. It's really just those areas because they're the parts everyone sees right away when I'm in town, you know? The rest of me doesn't bother me at all. \n\nAm I... am I answering your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need.\n\nDoctor: How much do these concerns about your face and hands interfere with your daily life or activities?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean, I still get all the daily chores done, I promise. I don't let it stop me from doing the actual physical work around the farm. \n\nBut, um... I guess it has been getting in the way of the business side of things a bit. I feel a little foolish admitting this, to be honest, because I know I should just push through it. But there have been times recently where I've actually missed calls with our bankers, and I've put off some pretty important farm decisions. It's just... I'd spend so much time trying to get myself looking acceptable enough to sit down in a room with them, and when I couldn't, I'd just find an excuse to cancel. \n\nAnd we also have this little retreat on the property, and we do some craft sales... I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't stomach the idea of people looking at those pictures and seeing how awful my skin looks. \n\nI know it's not right to let it affect our livelihood like that, and I really am trying my best not to let it get out of hand. Is that... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm explaining things alright for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do, or is it more that you worry what others might think of them?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know, honestly. I mean, my wife tells me I'm crazy, that I just look like a guy who's been out in the sun his whole life. And I want to agree with her, I really do. I know I shouldn't care so much about it. \n\nBut when I look in the mirror, it's just... it looks so rough to me. The cracks are so deep. I guess I do worry a lot about what other people think, especially the folks in town who don't work with their hands like I do. I just feel like they must see it the same way I do, you know? Like I look worn out or... or unkempt. \n\nI don't mean to sound like I'm obsessed with what people think of me. I know I should have thicker skin than that, no pun intended. But it just feels so obvious to me. Does that... does that answer your question? I'm sorry if I'm not making total sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands might not look as bad to others as they do to you?\n\nPatient: I... I suppose you're right. Logically, there has to be a possibility, right? I mean, like I said, my wife is always telling me that I just look like a normal man who's worked outdoors his whole life. And I want to believe her, I really do. I don't want to sit here and sound completely unreasonable to you. \n\nBut... well, I guess that's exactly what brings me in to see you. Even though I hear what she says, I just... I can't seem to get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. When I have to sit down with a banker or one of the county people, I just get this overwhelming feeling that they're staring right at the cracks, and the sun spots, and the sagging skin. I try to tell myself they aren't looking at it, but... it's just so hard to shake that feeling. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds a bit silly for a man my age to be so hung up on. Am I... am I answering your question the way you need me to? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your face and hands is different from how they actually appear to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess we're sort of circling around the same idea, aren't we? I... I don't want to waste your time repeating myself, I know you're very busy. \n\nBut yes, I suppose... I suppose I do know, deep down, that my perception might be a little skewed. I mean, if I'm being perfectly honest with myself—and with you—I know that when I look in the mirror, I'm probably zooming in on every little flaw in a way that a stranger walking down the street just wouldn't do. \n\nIt's just that the feeling is so strong, you know? Even when my brain tells me, \"Hey, the banker isn't staring at your hands,\" my stomach is still tied in knots because it feels like he is. \n\nI'm trying to be better about recognizing that, though. I really am. I know it's not productive to get so worked up over it. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying to give you a straight answer, I just... I want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you have what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your face and hands been constant, or do they vary from day to day?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... that's a good question. I'm... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really kept a close eye on whether it changes from one day to the next. \n\nI mean, I try my best not to dwell on it constantly, because I know I have work to do and I don't want to let it get in the way of my responsibilities. I like to think I just put my head down and get on with things. So maybe there are days where I'm just too busy to notice it as much? But... I really couldn't say for certain if it actually comes and goes, or if it's just always sort of there in the background. \n\nI'm sorry I don't have a more exact answer for you on that. I feel like I should know that. Is... is that okay? I can try to pay more attention to it this week and keep a log or something, if that's something that would be helpful for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worth as a person is tied to how you look, or is it more about how others might perceive you?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... that's a tough one. I mean, I certainly don't want to sit here and tell you that I'm completely shallow, you know? I was raised to believe that a man's worth comes from his hard work and how he provides for his family, and... and I really do try to live by that. I've always tried to be a good husband, a good provider. \n\nBut... I guess when I'm sitting across from someone in a nice office, I just... I worry they aren't seeing the hard work. I worry they just see this old, weathered guy who doesn't belong in their world. So maybe it's more about how I think they're judging me? I don't know if that makes sense. \n\nI'm sorry, I'm probably not explaining this very well. I just... I want to make sure I'm not giving you the impression that I'm completely obsessed with vanity. I'm really not. Does... does that answer your question the right way? I can try to explain it differently if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When you're worried about how your face and hands look, is it more about the actual appearance itself or the idea that others might be judging you for it?\n\nPatient: Well, gosh... I feel like I might not have done a very good job explaining myself just a minute ago, I'm sorry. I didn't mean to confuse things or make you have to ask it twice. \n\nTo be completely honest with you... I really don't know how to separate the two. I haven't ever really sat down and thought about whether it's just the... the actual weathering and the skin itself, or the other person looking at it. It all just sort of blends together into one big knot in my stomach, you know? \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I'm not trying to be difficult or dodge the question, I just... I honestly haven't noticed where the worry about the appearance ends and the worry about the judgment begins. Is it alright if I just tell you I'm not really sure about that one? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need for your notes.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your face and hands, is it the appearance itself that bothers you or the thought of what others might think when they see you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I feel terrible, I think you might be asking me the same thing again because I didn't give you a very good answer before. I'm really sorry if I'm making this difficult for you. \n\nI guess... I just really don't have a good way to pull those two things apart. I haven't ever stopped to think if it's the actual rough skin and the lines that upset me, or just the idea of someone else looking at them and thinking less of me. It's just... it's all one big feeling of not being good enough, I suppose. \n\nI feel awful that I can't give you a clearer answer. I really want to be helpful. Is it okay that I just... I really don't know? I can try to think on it harder if you need me to pick one or the other.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I didn't mean to confuse things or make you have to ask it twice. \n\nTo be completely honest with you... I really don't know how to separate the two. I haven't ever really sat down and thought about whether it's just the... the actual weathering and the skin itself, or the other person looking at it. It all just sort of blends together into one big knot in my stomach, you know? \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I'm not trying to be difficult or dodge the question, I just... I honestly haven't noticed where the worry about the appearance ends and the worry about the judgment begins. Is it alright if I just tell you I'm not really sure about that one? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need for your notes.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your face and hands, is it the appearance itself that bothers you or the thought of what others might think when they see you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I feel terrible, I think you might be asking me the same thing again because I didn't give you a very good answer before. I'm really sorry if I'm making this difficult for you. \n\nI guess... I just really don't have a good way to pull those two things apart. I haven't ever stopped to think if it's the actual rough skin and the lines that upset me, or just the idea of someone else looking at them and thinking less of me. It's just... it's all one big feeling of not being good enough, I suppose. \n\nI feel awful that I can't give you a clearer answer. I really want to be helpful. Is it okay that I just... I really don't know? I can try to think on it harder if you need me to pick one or the other.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you think others actually notice your face and hands, or is it just your worry about what they might think?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 62 / miscellaneous_agricultural_worker / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Preoccupation_with_Appearance\nmanifestation: \"What brings me in is that I can't get past how rough and worn my face and hands look, like every banker or county person is staring at the cracks, spots, and sagging even though my wife says I just look like a man who's worked outdoors.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I really hope I'm not sounding crazy or paranoid when I say this. I don't want you to think I'm the sort of fellow who thinks everyone is looking at him all the time. But... well, yes, I do think they notice. 

When I have to go into town, you know, to talk to the bankers or the county folks about the farm... I just feel like they're staring right at it. The cracks, the sun spots, the way my skin is sagging. I can't get past how rough and worn I look compared to them. My wife, bless her heart, she tries to tell me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, and maybe she's right... but I just can't shake the feeling that these professionals are looking at my face and hands and judging me for it. 

I'm sorry, does that make me sound terribly vain? I really don't mean to be. I just... I want to be honest with you about what's bringing me in, even if it's a little embarrassing to admit. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh... that's a really good question. I, uh... I don't know that I've ever really thought about it quite like that before. I'm sorry, I hope I'm not being too slow to answer.

If I was completely alone... well, I suppose I wouldn't have to worry about the bankers or the folks at the feed store looking at me, which would be a relief. But... I think I'd still see it when I looked in the mirror, wouldn't I? The deep lines and the rough patches. It's hard to say for sure since I'm usually out and about or with my wife, but... I don't think being by myself would just make the feeling go away entirely. 

I'm sorry, I know "I'm not sure" isn't a very helpful answer. Does that make sense at all, or am I just talking in circles? I can try to give you a firmer yes or no if that's what you need for your assessment.

Whenever I have to go into town and talk to the banker or the county folks, I just feel like they're staring right at all of it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a mountain out of a molehill, that I just look like a normal man who's worked outside his whole life. And I want to believe her, I really do. But it just bothers me so much. I hope it's okay that I'm bringing it up.\n\nDoctor: Is it just your face and hands that you're concerned about, or are there other parts of your body that bother you as well?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's really just... just my face and my hands. The rest of me is, you know, fine. Just normal for a guy my age, I suppose. \n\nI'm not trying to complain about my whole body or anything, I promise. I know I'm 62, I'm not expecting to look perfect. I really hope I don't come across as too vain or... or self-obsessed by focusing on this. I don't let it stop me from doing the actual physical work around the farm. \n\nBut, um... I guess it has been getting in the way of the business side of things a bit. I feel a little foolish admitting this, to be honest, because I know I should just push through it. But there have been times recently where I've actually missed calls with our bankers, and I've put off some pretty important farm decisions. It's just... I'd spend so much time trying to get myself looking acceptable enough to sit down in a room with them, and when I couldn't, I'd just find an excuse to cancel. \n\nAnd we also have this little retreat on the property, and we do some craft sales... I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't stomach the idea of people looking at those pictures and seeing how awful my skin looks. \n\nI know it's not right to let it affect our livelihood like that, and I really am trying my best not to let it get out of hand. Is that... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm explaining things alright for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do, or is it more that you worry what others might think of them?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know, honestly. I mean, my wife tells me I'm crazy, that I just look like a guy who's been out in the sun his whole life. And I want to agree with her, I really do. I know I shouldn't care so much about it. \n\nBut when I look in the mirror, it's just... it looks so rough to me. The cracks are so deep. I guess I do worry a lot about what other people think, especially the folks in town who don't work with their hands like I do. I just feel like they must see it the same way I do, you know? Like I look worn out or... or unkempt. \n\nI don't mean to sound like I'm obsessed with what people think of me. I know I should have thicker skin than that, no pun intended. But it just feels so obvious to me. Does that... does that answer your question? I'm sorry if I'm not making total sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands might not look as bad to others as they do to you?\n\nPatient: I... I suppose you're right. Logically, there has to be a possibility, right? I mean, like I said, my wife is always telling me that I just look like a normal man who's worked outdoors his whole life. And I want to believe her, I really do. I don't want to sit here and sound completely unreasonable to you. \n\nBut... well, I guess that's exactly what brings me in to see you. Even though I hear what she says, I just... I can't seem to get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. When I have to sit down with a banker or one of the county people, I just get this overwhelming feeling that they're staring right at the cracks, and the sun spots, and the sagging skin. I try to tell myself they aren't looking at it, but... it's just so hard to shake that feeling. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds a bit silly for a man my age to be so hung up on. Am I... am I answering your question the way you need me to? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your face and hands is different from how they actually appear to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess we're sort of circling around the same idea, aren't we? I... I don't want to waste your time repeating myself, I know you're very busy. \n\nBut yes, I suppose... I suppose I do know, deep down, that my perception might be a little skewed. I mean, if I'm being perfectly honest with myself—and with you—I know that when I look in the mirror, I'm probably zooming in on every little flaw in a way that a stranger walking down the street just wouldn't do. \n\nIt's just that the feeling is so strong, you know? Even when my brain tells me, \"Hey, the banker isn't staring at your hands,\" my stomach is still tied in knots because it feels like he is. \n\nI'm trying to be better about recognizing that, though. I really am. I know it's not productive to get so worked up over it. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying to give you a straight answer, I just... I want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you have what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your face and hands been constant, or do they vary from day to day?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... that's a good question. I'm... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really kept a close eye on whether it changes from one day to the next. \n\nI mean, I try my best not to dwell on it constantly, because I know I have work to do and I don't want to let it get in the way of my responsibilities. I like to think I just put my head down and get on with things. So maybe there are days where I'm just too busy to notice it as much? But... I really couldn't say for certain if it actually comes and goes, or if it's just always sort of there in the background. \n\nI'm sorry I don't have a more exact answer for you on that. I feel like I should know that. Is... is that okay? I can try to pay more attention to it this week and keep a log or something, if that's something that would be helpful for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worth as a person is tied to how you look, or is it more about how others might perceive you?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... that's a tough one. I mean, I certainly don't want to sit here and tell you that I'm completely shallow, you know? I was raised to believe that a man's worth comes from his hard work and how he provides for his family, and... and I really do try to live by that. I've always tried to be a good husband, a good provider. \n\nBut... I guess when I'm sitting across from someone in a nice office, I just... I worry they aren't seeing the hard work. I worry they just see this old, weathered guy who doesn't belong in their world. So maybe it's more about how I think they're judging me? I don't know if that makes sense. \n\nI'm sorry, I'm probably not explaining this very well. I just... I want to make sure I'm not giving you the impression that I'm completely obsessed with vanity. I'm really not. Does... does that answer your question the right way? I can try to explain it differently if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When you're worried about how your face and hands look, is it more about the actual appearance itself or the idea that others might be judging you for it?\n\nPatient: Well, gosh... I feel like I might not have done a very good job explaining myself just a minute ago, I'm sorry. I guess I do worry a lot about what other people think, especially the folks in town who don't work with their hands like I do. I just feel like they must see it the same way I do, you know? Like I look worn out or... or unkempt. \n\nI don't mean to sound like I'm obsessed with what people think of me. I know I should have thicker skin than that, no pun intended. But it just feels so obvious to me. Does that... does that answer your question? I'm sorry if I'm not making total sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands might not look as bad to others as they do to you?\n\nPatient: I... I suppose you're right. Logically, there has to be a possibility, right? I mean, like I said, my wife is always telling me that I just look like a normal man who's worked outdoors his whole life. And I want to believe her, I really do. I don't want to sit here and sound completely unreasonable to you. \n\nBut... well, I guess that's exactly what brings me in to see you. Even though I hear what she says, I just... I can't seem to get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. When I have to sit down with a banker or one of the county people, I just get this overwhelming feeling that they're staring right at the cracks, and the sun spots, and the sagging skin. I try to tell myself they aren't looking at it, but... it's just so hard to shake that feeling. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds a bit silly for a man my age to be so hung up on. Am I... am I answering your question the way you need me to? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your face and hands is different from how they actually appear to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess we're sort of circling around the same idea, aren't we? I... I don't want to waste your time repeating myself, I know you're very busy. \n\nBut yes, I suppose... I suppose I do know, deep down, that my perception might be a little skewed. I mean, if I'm being perfectly honest with myself—and with you—I know that when I look in the mirror, I'm probably zooming in on every little flaw in a way that a stranger walking down the street just wouldn't do. \n\nIt's just that the feeling is so strong, you know? Even when my brain tells me, \"Hey, the banker isn't staring at your hands,\" my stomach is still tied in knots because it feels like he is. \n\nI'm trying to be better about recognizing that, though. I really am. I know it's not productive to get so worked up over it. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying to give you a straight answer, I just... I want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you have what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your face and hands been constant, or do they vary from day to day?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... that's a good question. I'm... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really kept a close eye on whether it changes from one day to the next. \n\nI mean, I try my best not to dwell on it constantly, because I know I have work to do and I don't want to let it get in the way of my responsibilities. I like to think I just put my head down and get on with things. So maybe there are days where I'm just too busy to notice it as much? But... I really couldn't say for certain if it actually comes and goes, or if it's just always sort of there in the background. \n\nI'm sorry I don't have a more exact answer for you on that. I feel like I should know that. Is... is that okay? I can try to pay more attention to it this week and keep a log or something, if that's something that would be helpful for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worth as a person is tied to how you look, or is it more about how others might perceive you?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... that's a tough one. I mean, I certainly don't want to sit here and tell you that I'm completely shallow, you know? I was raised to believe that a man's worth comes from his hard work and how he provides for his family, and... and I really do try to live by that. I've always tried to be a good husband, a good provider. \n\nBut... I guess when I'm sitting across from someone in a nice office, I just... I worry they aren't seeing the hard work. I worry they just see this old, weathered guy who doesn't belong in their world. So maybe it's more about how I think they're judging me? I don't know if that makes sense. \n\nI'm sorry, I'm probably not explaining this very well. I just... I want to make sure I'm not giving you the impression that I'm completely obsessed with vanity. I'm really not. Does... does that answer your question the right way? I can try to explain it differently if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When you're worried about how your face and hands look, is it more about the actual appearance itself or the idea that others might be judging you for it?\n\nPatient: Well, gosh... I feel like I might not have done a very good job explaining myself just a minute ago, I'm sorry. I didn't mean to confuse things or make you have to ask it twice. \n\nTo be completely honest with you... I really don't know how to separate the two. I haven't ever really sat down and thought about whether it's just the... the actual weathering and the skin itself, or the other person looking at it. It all just sort of blends together into one big knot in my stomach, you know? \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I'm not trying to be difficult or dodge the question, I just... I honestly haven't noticed where the worry about the appearance ends and the worry about the judgment begins. Is it alright if I just tell you I'm not really sure about that one? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need for your notes.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your face and hands, is it the appearance itself that bothers you or the thought of what others might think when they see you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I feel terrible, I think you might be asking me the same thing again because I didn't give you a very good answer before. I'm really sorry if I'm making this difficult for you. \n\nI guess... I just really don't have a good way to pull those two things apart. I haven't ever stopped to think if it's the actual rough skin and the lines that upset me, or just the idea of someone else looking at them and thinking less of me. It's just... it's all one big feeling of not being good enough, I suppose. \n\nI feel awful that I can't give you a clearer answer. I really want to be helpful. Is it okay that I just... I really don't know? I can try to think on it harder if you need me to pick one or the other.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your face and hands, or is it just your worry about what they might think?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really hope I'm not sounding crazy or paranoid when I say this. I don't want you to think I'm the sort of fellow who thinks everyone is looking at him all the time. But... well, yes, I do think they notice. \n\nWhen I have to go into town, you know, to talk to the bankers or the county folks about the farm... I just feel like they're staring right at it. The cracks, the sun spots, the way my skin is sagging. I can't get past how rough and worn I look compared to them. My wife, bless her heart, she tries to tell me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, and maybe she's right... but I just can't shake the feeling that these professionals are looking at my face and hands and judging me for it. \n\nI'm sorry, does that make me sound terribly vain? I really don't mean to be. I just... I want to be honest with you about what's bringing me in, even if it's a little embarrassing to admit. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be bothered by the appearance of your face and hands if you were completely alone and nobody else could see you?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... that's a really good question. I, uh... I don't know that I've ever really thought about it quite like that before. I'm sorry, I hope I'm not being too slow to answer.\n\nIf I was completely alone... well, I suppose I wouldn't have to worry about the bankers or the folks at the feed store looking at me, which would be a relief. But... I think I'd still see it when I looked in the mirror, wouldn't I? The deep lines and the rough patches. It's hard to say for sure since I'm usually out and about or with my wife, but... I don't think being by myself would just make the feeling go away entirely. \n\nI'm sorry, I know \"I'm not sure\" isn't a very helpful answer. Does that make sense at all, or am I just talking in circles? I can try to give you a firmer yes or no if that's what you need for your assessment.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Does that make sense at all, or am I just talking in circles? I can try to give you a firmer yes or no if that's what you need for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Would your concerns about your face and hands bother you just as much if you never had to interact with anyone else?\n\nPatient: Oh dear... I'm so sorry, I must have really botched my last answer since you're having to ask me again. I feel awful, I'm trying my best not to make your job harder, but I feel like I'm failing a test here. \n\nTo be completely honest with you... I just really don't know. I've never lived a life where I didn't have to see anyone at all, you know? I've always had my wife, or the folks at the farm, or the people in town. So it's terribly hard to guess if it would bother me exactly as much, or a little less, or what. \n\nI wish I could give you a clearer answer, I really do, but I'm just coming up blank. I hope you're not frustrated with me. Is it alright if I just leave it at 'I don't know' for that one? I promise I'm not trying to be difficult.\n\n[Last Question]\nWould you say your concerns about your face and hands are more about how you feel about your own appearance or about how you think others perceive you?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 62 / miscellaneous_agricultural_worker / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, well... I hope I'm not giving you the wrong impression here. I really don't want to come across as someone who's just obsessed with what people think of him. I mean, a man my age shouldn't be so worried about other people's opinions, right? 

But... gosh, it's hard to separate the two. I suppose... well, I feel awful when I look in the mirror, but that feeling gets so much worse when I have to go into town and face the bankers. So maybe it's a bit of both? Or maybe I'm just confusing the issue again. I'm sorry, I feel like I'm not explaining this very well. 

Does that sound like a normal way to feel about it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the kind of information you need to understand what's going on with me. I can try to be more specific if that would help you.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Thank you for seeing me. I, um... I hope I'm not wasting your time with this. It's a little embarrassing to talk about, to be honest. I mean, I've worked outdoors in agriculture for years, so I know I shouldn't be so hung up on this, but... well, I just can't seem to get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. \n\nIt's the deep cracks, the sun spots, the sagging skin. Whenever I have to go into town and talk to the banker or the county folks, I just feel like they're staring right at all of it, judging me for it. My wife says I'm making a mountain out of a molehill, that I just look like a normal man who's worked outside his whole life. And I want to believe her, I really do. But it just bothers me so much. The rest of me is, you know, fine. Just normal for a guy my age, I suppose. \n\nI'm not trying to complain about my whole body or anything, I promise. I know I'm 62, I'm not expecting to look perfect. I really hope I don't come across as too vain or... or self-obsessed by focusing on this. I don't let it stop me from doing the actual physical work around the farm. \n\nBut, um... I guess it has been getting in the way of the business side of things a bit. But there have been times recently where I've actually missed calls with our bankers, and I've put off some pretty important farm decisions. It's just... I've completely stopped agreeing to be in any of the photos that would help promote it. I just can't stomach the idea of people looking at those pictures and seeing how awful my skin looks. \n\nI know it's not right to let it affect our livelihood like that, and I really am trying my best not to let it get out of hand. Is that... is that the kind of information you were looking for? I hope I'm explaining things alright for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually look as bad as you feel they do, or is it more that you worry what others might think of them?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I don't know, honestly. I mean, my wife tells me I'm crazy, that I just look like a guy who's been out in the sun his whole life. And I want to agree with her, I really do. I know I shouldn't care so much about it. \n\nBut when I look in the mirror, it's just... it looks so rough to me. The cracks are so deep. I guess I do worry a lot about what other people think, especially the folks in town who don't work with their hands like I do. I just feel like they must see it the same way I do, you know? Like I look worn out or... or unkempt. \n\nI don't mean to sound like I'm obsessed with what people think of me. I know I should have thicker skin than that, no pun intended. But it just feels so obvious to me. Does that... does that answer your question? I'm sorry if I'm not making total sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands might not look as bad to others as they do to you?\n\nPatient: I... I suppose you're right. Logically, there has to be a possibility, right? I mean, like I said, my wife is always telling me that I just look like a normal man who's worked outdoors his whole life. And I want to believe her, I really do. I don't want to sit here and sound completely unreasonable to you. \n\nBut... well, I guess that's exactly what brings me in to see you. Even though I hear what she says, I just... I can't seem to get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. When I have to sit down with a banker or one of the county people, I just get this overwhelming feeling that they're staring right at the cracks, and the sun spots, and the sagging skin. I try to tell myself they aren't looking at it, but... it's just so hard to shake that feeling. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds a bit silly for a man my age to be so hung up on. Am I... am I answering your question the way you need me to? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your face and hands is different from how they actually appear to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess we're sort of circling around the same idea, aren't we? I... I don't want to waste your time repeating myself, I know you're very busy. \n\nBut yes, I suppose... I suppose I do know, deep down, that my perception might be a little skewed. I mean, if I'm being perfectly honest with myself—and with you—I know that when I look in the mirror, I'm probably zooming in on every little flaw in a way that a stranger walking down the street just wouldn't do. \n\nIt's just that the feeling is so strong, you know? Even when my brain tells me, \"Hey, the banker isn't staring at your hands,\" my stomach is still tied in knots because it feels like he is. \n\nI'm trying to be better about recognizing that, though. I really am. I know it's not productive to get so worked up over it. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying to give you a straight answer, I just... I want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you have what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your face and hands been constant, or do they vary from day to day?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... that's a good question. I'm... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really kept a close eye on whether it changes from one day to the next. \n\nI mean, I try my best not to dwell on it constantly, because I know I have work to do and I don't want to let it get in the way of my responsibilities. I like to think I just put my head down and get on with things. So maybe there are days where I'm just too busy to notice it as much? But... I really couldn't say for certain if it actually comes and goes, or if it's just always sort of there in the background. \n\nI'm sorry I don't have a more exact answer for you on that. I feel like I should know that. Is... is that okay? I can try to pay more attention to it this week and keep a log or something, if that's something that would be helpful for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worth as a person is tied to how you look, or is it more about how others might perceive you?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... that's a tough one. I mean, I certainly don't want to sit here and tell you that I'm completely shallow, you know? I was raised to believe that a man's worth comes from his hard work and how he provides for his family, and... and I really do try to live by that. I've always tried to be a good husband, a good provider. \n\nBut... I guess when I'm sitting across from someone in a nice office, I just... I worry they aren't seeing the hard work. I worry they just see this old, weathered guy who doesn't belong in their world. So maybe it's more about how I think they're judging me? I don't know if that makes sense. \n\nI'm sorry, I'm probably not explaining this very well. I just... I want to make sure I'm not giving you the impression that I'm completely obsessed with vanity. I'm really not. Does... does that answer your question the right way? I can try to explain it differently if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When you're worried about how your face and hands look, is it more about the actual appearance itself or the idea that others might be judging you for it?\n\nPatient: Well, gosh... I feel like I might not have done a very good job explaining myself just a minute ago, I'm sorry. I didn't mean to confuse things or make you have to ask it twice. \n\nTo be completely honest with you... I really don't know how to separate the two. I haven't ever really sat down and thought about whether it's just the... the actual weathering and the skin itself, or the other person looking at it. It all just sort of blends together into one big knot in my stomach, you know? \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I'm not trying to be difficult or dodge the question, I just... I honestly haven't noticed where the worry about the appearance ends and the worry about the judgment begins. Is it alright if I just tell you I'm not really sure about that one? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need for your notes.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your face and hands, is it the appearance itself that bothers you or the thought of what others might think when they see you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I feel terrible, I think you might be asking me the same thing again because I didn't give you a very good answer before. I'm really sorry if I'm making this difficult for you. \n\nI guess... I just really don't have a good way to pull those two things apart. I haven't ever stopped to think if it's the actual rough skin and the lines that upset me, or just the idea of someone else looking at them and thinking less of me. It's just... it's all one big feeling of not being good enough, I suppose. \n\nI feel awful that I can't give you a clearer answer. I really want to be helpful. Is it okay that I just... I really don't know? I can try to think on it harder if you need me to pick one or the other.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your face and hands, or is it just your worry about what they might think?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... My wife, bless her heart, she tries to tell me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, and maybe she's right... but I just can't shake the feeling that these professionals are looking at my face and hands and judging me for it. \n\nI'm sorry, does that make me sound terribly vain? I really don't mean to be. I just... I want to be honest with you about what's bringing me in, even if it's a little embarrassing to admit. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, my... well, that would certainly be a miracle, wouldn't it? Just waking up and looking... normal. Not having to hide my hands or worry about all the deep lines and sun damage. 

But as for whether I'd still worry about what people think... gosh, I'd like to tell you that it would fix everything right up, but I really just don't know. I haven't even let myself imagine something like that, to be honest with you. I suppose I'd like to think the worry would completely go away, but since I can't actually test it out... I just can't say for certain. 

I'm so sorry, I feel like I'm giving you another 'I don't know' and I really hate to be unhelpful. You're asking such good questions and I feel like I'm just fumbling through them. Is it alright if I'm not entirely sure about that one? I just don't want to give you a made-up answer if that's not what you're looking for. I hope I'm still doing okay with all this.

I honestly haven't noticed where the worry about the appearance ends and the worry about the judgment begins. Is it alright if I just tell you I'm not really sure about that one? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need for your notes.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your face and hands, is it the appearance itself that bothers you or the thought of what others might think when they see you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I feel terrible, I think you might be asking me the same thing again because I didn't give you a very good answer before. I'm really sorry if I'm making this difficult for you. \n\nI guess... I just really don't have a good way to pull those two things apart. I haven't ever stopped to think if it's the actual rough skin and the lines that upset me, or just the idea of someone else looking at them and thinking less of me. It's just... it's all one big feeling of not being good enough, I suppose. \n\nI feel awful that I can't give you a clearer answer. I really want to be helpful. Is it okay that I just... I really don't know? I can try to think on it harder if you need me to pick one or the other.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your face and hands, or is it just your worry about what they might think?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really hope I'm not sounding crazy or paranoid when I say this. I don't want you to think I'm the sort of fellow who thinks everyone is looking at him all the time. But... well, yes, I do think they notice. \n\nWhen I have to go into town, you know, to talk to the bankers or the county folks about the farm... I just feel like they're staring right at it. The cracks, the sun spots, the way my skin is sagging. I can't get past how rough and worn I look compared to them. My wife, bless her heart, she tries to tell me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, and maybe she's right... but I just can't shake the feeling that these professionals are looking at my face and hands and judging me for it. \n\nI'm sorry, does that make me sound terribly vain? I really don't mean to be. I just... I want to be honest with you about what's bringing me in, even if it's a little embarrassing to admit. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be bothered by the appearance of your face and hands if you were completely alone and nobody else could see you?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... that's a really good question. I, uh... I don't know that I've ever really thought about it quite like that before. I'm sorry, I hope I'm not being too slow to answer.\n\nIf I was completely alone... well, I suppose I wouldn't have to worry about the bankers or the folks at the feed store looking at me, which would be a relief. But... I think I'd still see it when I looked in the mirror, wouldn't I? The deep lines and the rough patches. It's hard to say for sure since I'm usually out and about or with my wife, but... I don't think being by myself would just make the feeling go away entirely. \n\nI'm sorry, I know \"I'm not sure\" isn't a very helpful answer. Does that make sense at all, or am I just talking in circles? Oh, dear... I feel terrible, but I think... well, I think that's very similar to what we were just discussing, isn't it? About being alone and whether it would still bother me?

I really couldn't say for certain if it actually comes and goes, or if it's just always sort of there in the background. \n\nI'm sorry I don't have a more exact answer for you on that. I feel like I should know that. Is... is that okay? I can try to pay more attention to it this week and keep a log or something, if that's something that would be helpful for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worth as a person is tied to how you look, or is it more about how others might perceive you?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... that's a tough one. I'm sorry if I'm not making total sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your face and hands might not look as bad to others as they do to you?\n\nPatient: I... I suppose you're right. Logically, there has to be a possibility, right? I mean, like I said, my wife is always telling me that I just look like a normal man who's worked outdoors his whole life. And I want to believe her, I really do. I don't want to sit here and sound completely unreasonable to you. \n\nBut... well, I guess that's exactly what brings me in to see you. Even though I hear what she says, I just... I can't seem to get past how rough and worn my face and hands look. When I have to sit down with a banker or one of the county people, I just get this overwhelming feeling that they're staring right at the cracks, and the sun spots, and the sagging skin. I try to tell myself they aren't looking at it, but... it's just so hard to shake that feeling. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably sounds a bit silly for a man my age to be so hung up on. Am I... am I answering your question the way you need me to? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your face and hands is different from how they actually appear to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess we're sort of circling around the same idea, aren't we? I... I don't want to waste your time repeating myself, I know you're very busy. \n\nBut yes, I suppose... I suppose I do know, deep down, that my perception might be a little skewed. I mean, if I'm being perfectly honest with myself—and with you—I know that when I look in the mirror, I'm probably zooming in on every little flaw in a way that a stranger walking down the street just wouldn't do. \n\nIt's just that the feeling is so strong, you know? Even when my brain tells me, \"Hey, the banker isn't staring at your hands,\" my stomach is still tied in knots because it feels like he is. \n\nI'm trying to be better about recognizing that, though. I really am. I know it's not productive to get so worked up over it. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying to give you a straight answer, I just... I want to make sure I'm explaining it right so you have what you need.\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your face and hands been constant, or do they vary from day to day?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... that's a good question. I'm... I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. I haven't really kept a close eye on whether it changes from one day to the next. \n\nI mean, I try my best not to dwell on it constantly, because I know I have work to do and I don't want to let it get in the way of my responsibilities. I like to think I just put my head down and get on with things. So maybe there are days where I'm just too busy to notice it as much? But... I really couldn't say for certain if it actually comes and goes, or if it's just always sort of there in the background. \n\nI'm sorry I don't have a more exact answer for you on that. I feel like I should know that. Is... is that okay? I can try to pay more attention to it this week and keep a log or something, if that's something that would be helpful for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worth as a person is tied to how you look, or is it more about how others might perceive you?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... that's a tough one. I mean, I certainly don't want to sit here and tell you that I'm completely shallow, you know? I was raised to believe that a man's worth comes from his hard work and how he provides for his family, and... and I really do try to live by that. I've always tried to be a good husband, a good provider. \n\nBut... I guess when I'm sitting across from someone in a nice office, I just... I worry they aren't seeing the hard work. I worry they just see this old, weathered guy who doesn't belong in their world. So maybe it's more about how I think they're judging me? I don't know if that makes sense. \n\nI'm sorry, I'm probably not explaining this very well. I just... I want to make sure I'm not giving you the impression that I'm completely obsessed with vanity. I'm really not. Does... does that answer your question the right way? I can try to explain it differently if you need me to.\n\nDoctor: When you're worried about how your face and hands look, is it more about the actual appearance itself or the idea that others might be judging you for it?\n\nPatient: Well, gosh... I feel like I might not have done a very good job explaining myself just a minute ago, I'm sorry. I didn't mean to confuse things or make you have to ask it twice. \n\nTo be completely honest with you... I really don't know how to separate the two. I haven't ever really sat down and thought about whether it's just the... the actual weathering and the skin itself, or the other person looking at it. It all just sort of blends together into one big knot in my stomach, you know? \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I'm not trying to be difficult or dodge the question, I just... I honestly haven't noticed where the worry about the appearance ends and the worry about the judgment begins. Is it alright if I just tell you I'm not really sure about that one? I want to make sure I'm giving you what you need for your notes.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your face and hands, is it the appearance itself that bothers you or the thought of what others might think when they see you?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I feel terrible, I think you might be asking me the same thing again because I didn't give you a very good answer before. I'm really sorry if I'm making this difficult for you. \n\nI guess... I just really don't have a good way to pull those two things apart. I haven't ever stopped to think if it's the actual rough skin and the lines that upset me, or just the idea of someone else looking at them and thinking less of me. It's just... it's all one big feeling of not being good enough, I suppose. \n\nI feel awful that I can't give you a clearer answer. I really want to be helpful. Is it okay that I just... I really don't know? I can try to think on it harder if you need me to pick one or the other.\n\nDoctor: Do you think others actually notice your face and hands, or is it just your worry about what they might think?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I really hope I'm not sounding crazy or paranoid when I say this. I don't want you to think I'm the sort of fellow who thinks everyone is looking at him all the time. But... well, yes, I do think they notice. \n\nWhen I have to go into town, you know, to talk to the bankers or the county folks about the farm... I just feel like they're staring right at it. The cracks, the sun spots, the way my skin is sagging. I can't get past how rough and worn I look compared to them. My wife, bless her heart, she tries to tell me I just look like a man who's worked outdoors his whole life, and maybe she's right... but I just can't shake the feeling that these professionals are looking at my face and hands and judging me for it. \n\nI'm sorry, does that make me sound terribly vain? I really don't mean to be. I just... I want to be honest with you about what's bringing me in, even if it's a little embarrassing to admit. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still be bothered by the appearance of your face and hands if you were completely alone and nobody else could see you?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... that's a really good question. I, uh... I don't know that I've ever really thought about it quite like that before. I'm sorry, I hope I'm not being too slow to answer.\n\nIf I was completely alone... well, I suppose I wouldn't have to worry about the bankers or the folks at the feed store looking at me, which would be a relief. But... I think I'd still see it when I looked in the mirror, wouldn't I? The deep lines and the rough patches. It's hard to say for sure since I'm usually out and about or with my wife, but... I don't think being by myself would just make the feeling go away entirely. \n\nI'm sorry, I know \"I'm not sure\" isn't a very helpful answer. Does that make sense at all, or am I just talking in circles? I can try to give you a firmer yes or no if that's what you need for your assessment.\n\nDoctor: Would your concerns about your face and hands bother you just as much if you never had to interact with anyone else?\n\nPatient: Oh dear... I'm so sorry, I must have really botched my last answer since you're having to ask me again. I feel awful, I'm trying my best not to make your job harder, but I feel like I'm failing a test here. \n\nTo be completely honest with you... I just really don't know. I've never lived a life where I didn't have to see anyone at all, you know? I've always had my wife, or the folks at the farm, or the people in town. So it's terribly hard to guess if it would bother me exactly as much, or a little less, or what. \n\nI wish I could give you a clearer answer, I really do, but I'm just coming up blank. I hope you're not frustrated with me. Is it alright if I just leave it at 'I don't know' for that one? I promise I'm not trying to be difficult.\n\nDoctor: Would you say your concerns about your face and hands are more about how you feel about your own appearance or about how you think others perceive you?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the kind of information you need to understand what's going on with me. I can try to be more specific if that would help you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face and hands actually appear as bad to others as they do to you?\n\nPatient: Oh... well, that's a tough one. But... I think I'd still see it when I looked in the mirror, wouldn't I? The deep lines and the rough patches. It's hard to say for sure since I'm usually out and about or with my wife, but... Just waking up and looking... normal.
